He moves about like a monkey in the hands of a juggler.
3707. tātu aṟiyātāṉ pētai vaittiyaṉ.
He who does not know how to feel one's pulse is an empiric.
3708. tātum illaip pirātum illai.
No cause, no complaint.
3709. tāparam illā iḷaṅkoṭipōla.
As a tender creeper without a prop.
3710. tāmatam tāḻvukku ētu.
Delay will lead to ruin.
3711. tāmarai ilait taṇṇīr pōl tatumpukiṟāṉ.
He trembles like a drop of water on a lotus leaf.
3712. tāmpu aṟutal tōṇṭiyum pottal.
The cord is rotten and the water pot is fractured.
3713. tāyum takappaṉum taḷḷiviṭṭa kālattil vā eṉṟu aḻaitta vaṅkāravāci.
A herb that welcomed a child cast off by its parents.
3714. tāyum piḷḷaiyum āṉālum vāyum vayiṟum vēṟē.
Though they are mother and child their mouths and bellies are diverse.
3715. tāyaik koṉṟavaṉukku ūrilē vāti.
The prosecutor of a matricide is in his own village.
3716. tāyaic cērnta uṟavu āṉālum aṟuttuttāṉ uṟavu āṭa vēṇṭum.
Though related on the side of one's mother he must be treated as a
relation after
thoroughly ascertaining his connections.
3717. tāyaippārttup peṇṇaik koḷḷu pālaip pārttup pacuvaik koḷḷu.
Before taking a woman in marriage ascertain the character of her mother,
beforebuying a cow ascertain the quantity of its milk.
3718. tāyaippōṟ piḷḷai nūlaippōṟ cīlai.
As is the mother such is the child, as is the yarn such is the cloth.
3719. tāyaip paḻittu makaḷ apacāri āṭukiṟāḷ.
The daughter reproached her mother and went astray.
3720. tāyaip pārttu makaḷaik koḷ.
Look at the mother before you take her daughter in marriage.
3721. tāy aṟiyāc cūl uṇṭō ?
Is there any conception unknown to the mother?
3722. tāy illāp piḷḷaiyait talaiyilē taṭṭalāmā?
Is it right to strike a motherless child on the head?
3723. tāy uṟavō nāy uṟavō .
A mother's attachment, a dogs attachment.
3724. tāy ēḻaṭi pāyntāl makaḷ eṭṭu aṭi pāyvāḷ.
If the mother leaps seven feet, the daughter leaps eight.
3725. tāy oru pākku, tāṉ kamukantōppu eṉkiṟāḷ.
She says that her mother is only a nut, while she herself is a grove of
arica-nut trees.
3726. tāy kēṭṭuppaṭṭi takappaṉ kāvaṭippaṭṭi taṅkai mōruppaṭṭi tamakkai cāttappaṭṭi.
The mother is wicked, the father vain, the younger sister a consumer of
butter milk, and the elder sister a consumer of rice.
3727. tāy kaiyil irukkiṟa taṉattaip pārkkilum taṉ kait taviṭē mēl.
The bran in one's own hand is preferable to the wealth in the hand of
his mother.
3728. tāykku uḷḷatu makaḷukku.
That which is the mother's, is the daughter's.
This may refer to prosperity, temper, &c.
3729. tāykkuppiṉ tāram.
Next to one's mother, is his wife.
3730. tāykku oḷitta cūlum uṇṭō ?
Can the conception of an unmarried daughter be concealed from her
mother?
3731. tāykku viḷaintālum taṉakkum viḷaiyavēṇṭum.
It is not enough for one's mother's field to bear a crop, one's own
field must also be fruitful.
3732. tāykkuc cōṟu iṭukiṟatu ūrukkup pukaḻcciyā?
Does one acquire fame in a country because he feeds his mother?
3733. tāykku mūttut takappaṉukku viḷakkup piṭikkiṟāṉ.
He carries a torch at his father's second marriage where the bride is
younger than himself.
3734. tāy cettāl maṇam makaḷ cettāl piṇam.
If the mother die, a marriage, if the daughter die, a corpse.
The wife is here called mother with reference to her children. In
the event of her death the husband may marry again.
3735. tāy cettāḷ, makaḷ tikkaṟṟāḷ.
The mother is dead, the daughter is destitute.
3736. tāy col tuṟantāl vācakam illai.
When one rejects the advice of his mother, no precept can reform him.
3737. tāy taviṭṭukku aḻukaiyil piḷḷai iñcip paccaṭi kēṭkiṉṟatu.
While the mother is crying for bran, the child is crying for ginger
chutny.
3738. tāy taṉakku ākāta makaḷ ārtaṉakku nallavaḷ āvāḷ?
Who will approve of a daughter that is undutiful to her own mother?
3739. tāy tūṟṟiṉāl ūr tūṟṟum, koṇṭavaṉ tūṟṟiṉāl kaṇṭavaṉ tūṟṟuvāṉ.
If a mother should spread evil reports, the village also will do so; if
a husband should defame his wife, a stranger will.
3740. tāy mukam kāṇāta piḷḷaiyum maḻai mukam kāṇāta payirum cevvaippaṭamāṭṭātu.
The child that has not seen the face of its mother, and the growing crop
that has not seen rain, will not do well.
3741. tāy mulaippālukkup pāl māṟiṉatu pōla.
As one complained that his mother's milk was insipid.
3742. tāy vārttai kēḷāta piḷḷai nāy vāyiṟ cīlai.
A child disobedient to her mother, is like a cloth in the mouth of a
dog.
3743. tāramum kuruvum talaiyil viti.
A wife and a Guru are preordained.
3744. tārāp peṭṭai pōla.
Like a duck.
3745. tārāḷantāṉ ney, taṇṇīr pantalil, nīrccōṟṟut taṇṇīr neypaṭṭa pāṭu.
Ghee is plentiful in his feasts, while cold rice water in the charity
pandal is as scarce as ghee.
3746. tāli aṟuttavaḷukku maruttuvicci tayavu ēṉ ?
Of what use is the favour of a midwife to a widow?
3747. tāli aṟuttavaḷ ēṉ irukkiṟāḷ? tāram tappiṉavaṉukkup poṅkiyiṭa.
Why does the widow survive her husband? that she may cook rice for a
widower.
3748. tāvat tañcam illā iḷaṅkoṭi pōl tavikkiṟāṉ.
He languishes like a tender creeper without a prop.
3749. tāḻipōl vayiṟum ūcipōl miṭaṟum.
Pot-bellied and needle-throated.
3750. tāḻ kulattiṟ piṟantālum puttiyiṉāl alarippūvaippōl pirayōcaṉappaṭuvār.
Though low-born, they may by their good sense prove useful as an
oleander flower.
3751. tāḻntu niṉṟāl vāḻntu niṟpāy.
If humble thou shalt prosper.
3752. tāḻntatu taṅkam uyarntatu pittaḷai.
Gold has become low in estimation, and brass high.
3753. tāḻntu paṇitalē talaimai ākum.
The humble and obedient shall rise to eminence.
3754. tāḻmai illāta vālipaṉ vīṇ.
An unsubmissive youth is useless.
3755. tāḻvilē perumaiyum vāḻvilē tāḻmaiyum vēṇṭum.
In adversity, manly bearing; in prosperity, humility.
3756. tāṉattaṉattāṉ cakala campattaṉ.
He is the wealthiest in the place, and possesses all in abundance.
3757. tāṉamatu virumpu.
Desire to be charitable.
3758. tāṉāka vanta cītēviyaik kālāl utaittut taḷḷalāmā?
When the goddess of prosperity unsought visits you, is it right tokick
her out?
3759. tāṉum uṇṇāṉ piṟarukkum koṭāṉ .
He will neither eat himself, nor give to others.
3760. tāṉē kaṉiyāta paḻattait taṭikoṇṭu aṭittāl kaṉiyumā?
Can you mature a fruit by beating it with a stick when it does not ripen
of itself?
3761. tāṉē tāṉ kurukkaḷ eṉpār taṉaṅkaḷ vāṅkac catācivaṉ pēr pūcai ceyvār.
To get money they call themselves gurus, and perform pujas in honour of
Sadasiva.
3762. tāṉē vaḷarntu tavattāl koṭi eṭuttavaṉ.
He has grown great and distinguished by his penance.
3763. tāṉē vāḻavēṇṭum talaimakaḷē aṟukkavēṇṭum eṉkiṟāḷ.
She herself desires to prosper, and wishes that her first-born daughter
may become a widow.
3764. tāṉ aṭaṅkat taṉ kulam aṭaṅkum.
If he himself be under restraint, his race will be so.
3765. tāṉ aṟiyāc ciṅkaḷam taṉ piṭarikkuc cētam.
An attempt to speak a language not understood may lead to one's own
hurt.
3766. tāṉ uḷḷapōtu ulakam.
While one lives, the world subserves him.
3767. tāṉ oṉṟu niṉaittāl teyvam oṉṟu niṉaikkum.
If he thinks one thing, Deity thinks another.
3768. tāṉ kaḷvaṉ piṟarai nampāṉ.
Himself a thief, he trusts not others.
3769. tāṉ kaṟṟa oṉṟait tarikka urai.
What you have learnt, teach to others impressively.
3770. tāṉ keṭuttatu pāti, tampirāṉ keṭuttatu pāti.
Half was spoiled by himself, and half by his superior.
3771. tāṉ campātittāl taṉakku utavum, ūr campātittāl utavamāṭṭātu.
That which one acquires will be available, that which the country
acquires will not be so.
3772.tāṉ cāka maruntu uṇpār illai.
No one takes medicine with a view to death.
3773. tāṉ cettapiṉ ulakam kaviḻntu eṉṉa nimirntu eṉṉa?
When one is dead, what matters it-whether the world be overturned or
not?
3774.tāṉ taḷampal piṟarukku ūṉṟukōl.
His tripping is as a staff to others.
3775. tāṉ tiruṭi ayal vīṭṭai nampāḷ.
Being herself a thief, she trusts not her neighbours.
3776.tāṉ tiṉkiṟa nañcu taṉṉaittāṉ kollum.
He who takes poison will destroy himself.
3777. tāṉ tiṉṉat taviṭu illai. vārattukkup paṉṟik kuṭṭiyā?
He has no bran to eat, why seek a young pig to rear for hire ?
3778. tāṉ tiṉṉit tampirāṉāy irukkiṟāṉ.
In eating he has a monopoly.
3779. tāṉ tiṉṉi piḷḷai vaḷarkkāḷ , taviṭu tiṉṉi kōḻi vaḷarkkāḷ.
A gluttonous mother willnot feed her child, nor will one who feeds on
bran keep
fowls.
3780. tāṉtiruṭṭuk koṭuttatum allāmal payittiyakkārap paṭṭamum kēṭṭāṉ.
Besides suffering the loss of the property stolen, he acquires the title
of a fool.
3781. tāṉ tēṭāp poṉṉukku māṟṟum illai uraiyum illai.
Gold not acquired by one's own exertions, has neither standard nor
touch.
3782. tāṉ pattiṉi āṉāl tēvaṭiyāḷ teruvilum kuṭi irukkalām.
If a woman be chaste she may live in the street of the harlets.
3783. tāṉ piṭitta mucalukku mūṉṟē kāl eṉkiṟāṉ.
He says that the hare he caught had three legs.
3784. tāṉ pōṉāl tākattukkuk kiṭaiyātu, eḻutaṭā ōlai nūṟukuṭam tayirukku
eṉkiṟāṉ.
He says that if he go himself he cannot get water to quench his
thirst, but if he sends written olas he can obtain a hundred pots of
curds.
3785. tāṉ pōkiṟa kāriyattukku āṭpōṉāl oru coṭṭu.
Faults will happen if another be deputed to do one's business.
3786. tāṉ pōy mōr illāmal vantavaṉ tayirukkuc cīṭṭu eḻutiṉāṉām.
It is said that he who went in person and could not obtain even
butter-milk, sent a written order for curds.
3787. tāṉ pōka vaḻiyaik kāṇāta mūñcūṟu viḷakku, māṟṟaiyum kavvikkoṇṭu pōṉatām.
It is said that a musk-rat that was not able to find its way out,
carried away the broom.
3788. tāṉṟik kāyil caṉiyaṉ pukuntatu pōla.
As if Saturn entered into the tanri fruit-Terminalia Bellerica.
3789. tikampara canniyācikku vaṇṇāṉ uṟavu ēṉ ?
What has a naked mendicant to do with the friendship of a washer- man?
3790. tikaippūṭu mitittavaṉ pōl alaikiṟāṉ.
He wanders about like one who has trodden on the tigai plant.
A plant whose touch is supposed to create aberration of mind and
numbness.
3791. tikku aṟṟavaṉukkut teyvam tuṇai.
God in the help of the destitute.
3792. tikku vijayam koḷpavaṉukku jeyam apajeyakālam teriyātu.
A successful conqueror does not regard times whether relating to victory
or defeat.
3793. tiṅkaḷum caṉiyum teṟkē nōkka vēṇṭum.
Look south on Monday and Saturday if leaving home.
The direction given in this proverb is intended to convey a caution to
those setting out on a journey on Monday or Saturday. It is based on the
belief contained in a stanza of which the following is a translation.
Sangara, Siva, as the author of good, plants his trident on the earthin
certain directions on particular days and at stated hours. On Monday and
Saturday in the east for eight naligais*.On Thursday, in the south for
twenty naligais. On Friday and Sunday in the west, twelve naligais. On
Tuesday and Wednesday in the north, twelve naligais.
* Indian hour of twenty-four minutes.
3794. tiṭamaṉappaṭu tīmparkkarukil.
Preserve your self-possession in the presence of enemies.
3795. tiṇṇaikkut tēḷ koṭṭiṟṟu taṇṇīr miṭāvukku neṟi kaṭṭiṟṟu eṉṟāṟpōla.
As if one should say that the pial was stung by a scorpion, and the
water pot standing on it became inflamed.
3796. tiṇṇaikku viṭintāl vīṭṭukkum viṭiyum.
If the sun rise on the pial, it will rise on the house also.
3797. tiraviyattil aḻuttamāṉavaṉ cettālum koṭāṉ.
The miserly will not give though threatened with immediate death.
3798. tiraḷ eli vaḷai eṭātu.
Rats in large numbers do not burrow.
3799. tirittavaraiyiṟ kayiṟu tiriyātavaraiyiṟ paḻutai.
As far as twisted it is a rope, that not twisted is mere fibre.
3800. tiri mūrttikaḷum tēvarum kāṇār.
Neither the triad nor the thirty-three crores of supernals, are
comparable to him.
3801. tiru uṇṭāṉāl tiṟamai uṇṭākum.
When there is wealth, there is power.
3802. tirukāṇikku valuvum paḻañcāṇikkup puḻuvum uṇṭu.
A screw is strong, old cow-dung breeds worms.
3803. tiruk kaṇṭa kaṇṇukkut tīṅkum kāṇalāyiṟṟē.
Even those who have seen the holy one are not exempt from evil.
3804. tiruk kaṇṭa kaṇṇukkut tīṅku illai.
Those that have seen the holy one experience no evil.
3805. tirukkāvaṇap pantalukku niḻal utavi vēṇṭumā?
Does a marriage pandal require shading?
3806. tiruṭaṉait tēḷ koṭṭiṉatu pōla.
As a scorpion stung the thief.
3807. tiruṭaṉ peṇ cāti eṉṟaikkum kaimpeṇcāti.
The wife of the thief is always a widow.
3808. tiruṭaṉukkut teyvamē cāṭci.
The Deity is witness against the thief.
3809. tiruṭṭup payalukkut tiraṭṭuppālum cōṟum vicuvācakkāraṉukku vennīrum
parukkaiyum.
A rogue is fed with thick milk and rice, while the honest get nothing
but warm water and rice.
3810. tiruṭṭu nāykkuc calaṅkai kaṭṭiṉāṟ pōla.
Like tying a string of bells round the neck of a thievish dog.
3811. tiruṭṭup payal kaliyāṇattil muṭiccu aviḻkkiṟa payal periyataṉam.
At the marriage of a thief, the pickpocket is the chief guest.
Literally, the thievish
fellow who unties a knot. Money or other valuables being often carried
in the corner of the cloth worn as dress, or in the corner of a
handkerchief.
3812. tiruṭṭup pūṉaikkup pōṭu tiraṭṭup pālum cōṟum.
Put thick milk and rice before a thievish cat.
3813. tirutti illāta ecamāṉ vīṇ.
A master whom it is hard to please is useless.
3814. tirunāḷum muṭintatu eṭupiṭiyum kaḻintatu.
The festival is over, the bustle has ceased.
3815. tirunāḷukku pōkiṟaiyō tiṉṉutalukkup pōkiṟaiyō?
Do you go to celebrate the holy day, or for the sake of food?
3816. tirunīṟṟilē oṭṭātu kaḻaṟcikkāy.
Holy ashes will not adhere to a killachi-kai.
3817. tirunta ōtat tiru uṇṭāmē.
The correct utterance of mantras secures the divine favoar.
3818. tiruppatikkup pōṉālum tuṭaippam oru kācu.
Though taken to Tripati, a broom will fetch only a cash.
3819. tiruppatiyil moṭṭai aṭittatum paṟṟātā, srīraṅkattil cirippāyc cirittatum
paṟṟātā?
Is it not enough to have been shaven bald at Tirupati and disgraced at
Shrirangam?
3820. tiruppatiyil moṭṭait tātaṉaik kaṇṭāyā?
Did you see the baldheaded Vaishnava mendicant at Tripati ?
3821. tiruvaṉ kaṇṭa paccaiyāyp pōyiṟṟu.
It has become an emerald discovered by the king's jester.
3822. tiru vācal āṇṭiyum oru vēḷaikku utavuvāṉ.
Even a religious mendicant at the temple gate will be of some use at
times.
3823. tiruvācakattiṟku urukātār oru vācakattiṟkum urukār.
Those who are unaffected by Tiruvasakam will not be moved by any other
composition.
3824. tiruvākkukku etir vākku uṇṭō ?
Do the words of the great admit of contradiction ?
3825. tiruviḷakku illā vīṭṭil pēy kuṭiyirukkum.
Demons dwell in the house that is not illumined by a sacred lamp.
To smear a room with cow-dung, especially on Friday, and to keep a lamp
burning through the night, ale observances pleasing to the goddess of
prosperity.
3826. tiruviḷakku iṭṭārait teyvam aṟiyum, neyvārttu uṇṭārai neñcu aṟiyum.
The Deity knows those who place sacred lamps, and the mind knows who
eats ghee and rice.
3827. tirai kaṭal ōṭiyum tiraviyam tēṭu.
Acquire wealth though compelled to cross the stormyocean.
3828. tillumullum tiriyā varamum.
Lies and tricks.
3829. tiṟanta katavukku tiṟavukōl tēṭuvāṉēṉ?
Why seek the key of an open door ?
3830. tiṟanta vīṭṭilē nāy nuḻaintāṟpōl.
As if a dog entered an open house.
3831. tiṉattava nilaiyil maṉattai niṟuttu,
Daily fix your mind on divine things.
3832. tiṉavukkuc coṟital itam.
Scratching is agreeable where itching exists.
3833. tiṉavu eṭuttavaṉ coṟintu koḷḷa vēṇṭum.
He whose skin itches will scratch.
3834. tiṉaippayaṟum pālum tiṉṉātiruntum, viṉaippayaṉai velluvatu aritu.
Though one may abstain from eating millet-pulse and milk, he cannot
escape from the effect of his evil actions.
3835. tiṉai vitaittavaṉ tiṉai aṟuppāṉ viṉai vitaittavaṉ viṉai aṟuppāṉ.
He who sows millet, reaps millet, he who sows evil deeds, must reap the
same.
3836. tiṉkiṟatait tiṉṟum tēvāṅkupōl irukkiṟāṉ.
Though well fed he is as lean as a sloth.
3837. tiṉpatu koñcam cīvaṉam nilai illai.
What one eats is little, human life is uncertain.
3838. tiṉṟa maṇṇukkut takka cōkai.
Chogai jaundice proportioned to the earth eaten.
It is the opinion of some that jaundice is occasioned by eating sand or
earth, a thing not uncommon.
3839. tiṉṟa nañcu kollum tiṉṉāta nañcu kollumā?
Poison taken kills; will the poison not taken, kill?
3840. tiṉṟu koḻuttāl cummā irukka oṭṭātu.
He who has grown fat will be inclined to mischief.
3841. tiṉṟu umiḻnta tampalattait tiṉṉa niṉaippārkaḷā?
Does any one desire to chew his betel over again?
3842. tiṉṉat tiṉṉat kēṭkumām piḷḷaippeṟṟa vayiṟu.
A mother who is nursing a b by has a good appetite.
3843. tiṉṉat teriyāmal tiṉpāṉēṉ?
Why eat, seeing that you know not how to eat with moderation?
3844. tiṉṉā vīṭṭil tiṉṉi.
One who takes food in a house in which he ought not.
3845. tīk kāyntāṟpōl irukkavēṇṭum.
Act as one who warms himself-do not burn yourself.
3846. tīkkuk kāṟṟu utaviyāṉatupōla.
As the wind assists fire.
3847. tīṅkarumpaṉukku māṅkuyiṟ kiḷavi.
The notes of the kuyil bird to the sugar-cane-bowed Cupid.
3848. tīṭṭiṉ marattil kūrpārkkiṟatā ?
Is the sharpness of the sword to be tried on the whetting board ?
3849. tītuṟum pāvac ceykai aṟṟavaṉ tēvaṉ.
He who abstains from evil deeds is God.
3850. tīpattil ēṟṟiya tīvaṭṭi.
A torch lit at a lamp.
3851. tīppaṭṭa vīṭṭilē karikkaṭṭai pañcamā?
Is there a lack of charred wood in a house on fire ?
3852. tīppuṇ āṟum vāyppuṇ āṟātu.
A burn is curable, but a wound occasioned by slander is not.
3853. tī miñca vaittālum pakai miñca vaikkalākātu.
Though fire may be in excess, hatred may not.
3854. tīmaiyai meccukiṟavaṉ tīmaiyāḷitāṉ.
He who approves evil is guilty of it.
3855. tīyāraic cērntu oḻukal tītu, tīyār paṇ ceyvaṉavum tītu.
To associate with the wicked is bad, to serve the wicked is also bad.
3856. tīyōrai viṭutalai ākkukiṟavaṉ nallōrukku naṣṭam ceyvāṉ.
He who liberates the wicked injures the innocent.
3857. tīrak kaṟṟavaṉ tēcikaṉ āvāṉ.
The thoroughly learned may become a religious guide.
3858. tīrāk kōpam pāṭāy muṭiyum.
Unrestrained anger will end in mischief.
3859. tīrāk kōpam pōrāy muṭiyum.
Unrestrained anger ends in strife.
3860. tīrāc cantēkam pōrukku ettaṉam.
Continued uncertainty leads to war.
3861. tīrāc ceykai cīrākātu.
An unsettled affair is bad.
3862. tīrāta neñcukkut teyvamē cāṭci.
God himself is the witness of the unquiet mind.
3863. tīrā vaḻakkukkut teyvamē cāṭci.
God is the witness in an undecided cause.
3864. tīrā vaḻakku nērākātu.
An intricate case will never end satisfactorily.
3865. tīviṉai ceyyil pēyviṉai ceyyum.
A demon does evil to him who does evil to others.
3866. tukkam uḷḷa maṉatiṟkut tuṉpam ēṉ vēṟu ?
Why additional pain to a mind already in grief?
3867. tuñci niṉṟāṉ miñci uṇṇāṉ.
He that is addicted to sleep loses his appetite.
3868. tuṭiyāp peṇṭir maṭiyil neruppu.
Unsympathizing wives are like fire in the bosom.
3869. tuṭuppu irukkak kai vēvāṉēṉ?
Why should the hand be burnt when there is a ladle ?
3870. tuṭaippak kaṭṭaikkup paṭṭukkuñcam kaṭṭiṉatupōla.
Like tying a silk tassel to a broomstick.
3871. tuṣṭa nikkirakam ciṣṭa paripālaṉam.
To the wicked, punishment, to the good, protection.
3872. tuṣṭap piḷḷaikku ūrār putti colvārkaḷ.
The people of the village will reprove a wicked child.
3873. tuṭṭukku oru kuṭṭi viṟṟālum tulukkakkuṭṭi māttiram ākātu.
Though girls may be had at a pie a head, a Muhammadan girl is
undesirable.
3874. tuṇikiṟavaḷukku veṭkam illai, aḻukiṟavaḷukkut tukkam illai.
An impudent woman has no shame, a weeping woman has nosorrow.
3875. tuṇintārkkut tukkam uṇṭā paṇintārkkup pāṭuuṇṭā ?
Do the adventurous experience grief, or the humble, distress?
3876. tuṇintavaṉukkut tukkam illai , aḻutavaṉukku akaṅkāram illai.
The adventurous know not sorrow, the sorrowful know not anger.
3877. tuṇaipōṉālum piṇaipōkātē.
Though you may bear one company, do not become his security.
3878. tuṇaiyōṭallatu vaḻipōkātē.
Do not go on a journey without a companion.
3879. tuppākki vayiṟṟil pīraṅki piṟantatupōla.
As a cannon was born of a musket.
3880. tumpar ampalam.
The wicked will be abandoned.
3881. tuyarappaṭṭāl āṟutal uṇṭu , tuṉpappaṭṭāl tēṟutal uṇṭu.
Grief leads to comfort, suffering to patience.
3882. turiyōtaṉaṉ kuṭikkuc cakuṉipōla.
Like Saguni to the family of Duryodhana.
Saguni was the maternal uncle of Duriyodhana, and had, like the latter,
ninety-nine brothers. Duriyodhana was one of the kings whose exploits
are narrated in the Epic Mahabharata. He imprisoned Saguni and his
ninety-nine brothers to prevent their combination against him, feeding
them on gruel which was doled out from day to day. They all died of
inanition excepting Saguni, who managed to secure sufficient nourishment
to preserve his life. He afterwards became the counsellor of Duriyodhana
and from sinister motives gavesuch advice as led to the ruin of his
royal master, who lost his kingdom and also his ninety-nine brothers.
3883. turukka teruvilē tiruvempāvaiyā ōtukiṟatu?
Is it in a Muhammadan street that one should recite Tiruvemba?
3884. turumpum kalattaṇṇīr tēkkum.
Even a fibre may help to fill up a kalam with water.
3885. turumpu nuḻaiya iṭam iruntāl āṉaiyaik kaṭṭuvāṉ.
If he find room for the insertion of a straw, he will tie an elephant.
3886. turumpu muṟṟiṉa kōpam vicumpu muṭṭat tīrum.
Anger occasioned by a trifle may extend to the sky before it ceases.
3887. turumpu tūṇ āṉāl tūṇ evvaḷavu ākavēṇṭum?
If a straw become a pillar, how great the pillar must be ?
3888. turumpait tūṇākkukiṟatā?
What, do you make a pillar of a straw?
3889. turai cittam kaṉa cittam.
The will of the ruler is weighty.
3890.turōkattāl koṇṭa turaittaṉam, kuṭikaḷai varuttum koṭuṅkōl.
Power obtained by high treason, a sceptre that oppresses the subject.
3891. turōpatayait tukil urintatu pōla.
Like stripping off the cloth of Draupati.
Draupati was the common wife of the five sons of Pandu. When in
captivity after the humiliation of the Pandavas, an attempt was made by
Duriyodhana to strip off her cloth, as a punishment for laughing at him
when he stumbled, she was however saved from dishonour, for as they drew
away her cloth it was lengthned by divine interposition.
3892. turccaṉaṉaik kaṇṭāl tūra nil.
Stand at a distance from the wicked.
3893. tulakkāta āyutam turup piṭikkum.
A weapon not kept polished, will become rusty.
3894. tulāttil veḷḷi iruppatiṉāl ulāvip peyyum maḻai.
Venus being in Libra, there will be rain throughout the country.
3895. tulukka teruvilē tēvāram ōtiṉatupōla.
Like singing Devaram in a Muhammadan street.
3896. tuvi nākku iṭaṟum.
A double tongue will slip.
3897. tuḻāvik kāyccātatu kañciyum alla, viṉāvik kaṭṭātatu kaliyāṇamum alla.
Kanji requires continued stirring while boiling, and marriage requires
careful consideration before being entered on.
3898. tuḷacikku vācamum muḷḷukkuk kūrmaiyum muḷaikkiṟapōtē uṇṭu.
The fragrance of the Tulasi and the point of a thorn are in the bud.
3899. tuḷḷātē tuḷḷātē kuḷḷā, pakkattilē paḷḷam aṭā.
Dwarf, do not jump, there is a ditch close by.
3900. tuḷḷātē tuḷḷātē āṭṭukkuṭṭi eṉ kaiyil irukkiṟatu cūrikkatti.
Do not leap about O lamb, the butcher's knife is in my hand.
3901. tuḷḷiṉa māṭu poti cumakkum.
The restive or unruly bullockwill carry its load.
3902. tuḷḷit tuḷḷik kutittālum veḷḷippaṇamum kiṭaiyāk kālattil kiṭaiyātu.
Though one may exert himself to the utmost, he cannot procure even a
silver fanam when fortune does not smile.
3903. tuḷḷit tuḷḷit toppeṉṟu viḻukiṟāy.
By restlessness thou wilt throw thyself headlong.
3904. tuḷḷumāṉ kuṭṭi turavil viḻuntatu.
The springing young deer fell into a well.
3905. tuṟaṭṭukku eṭṭātatu kaikku eṭṭumā?
Can that which is not reached by a long pole be seized by the hand?
3906. tuṟavaṟam illaṟam maṉatilē.
Ascetic and domestic virtues originate in the heart.
3907. tuṟavikku vēntaṉ turumpu.
A king is but a strawbefore an ascetic.
3908. tuṉpam tarukiṟa kākkaiyiṉatu cattattiṉāl atai viraṭṭuvārkaḷ, iṉpam tarukiṉṟa
kuyilai viraṭṭārkaḷ.
Men scare away crows because their cries are a nuisance, but cuckoos
they do not scare away.
3909. tuṉpattiṟku iṭam koṭēl.
Take care to keep off sickness.
3910. tuṉpam munti iṉpam pinti.
Pain precedes, pleasure follows.
3911. tūkkaṇaṅkuruvi kuraṅkiṟkup putti coṉṉāṟpōl.
As the hanging-nest bird gave advice to the monkey.
The story referred to is graphically told in, I think, Panchatantra. It
is as follows : On a cold rainy day a monkey chilled by the inclemency
of the weather approached a tree on the branches of which were hanging
numerous nests of this ingenious bird. One of the inmates addressed his
simian denizen of the forest expressing surprise that a creature endowed
with limbs so well fitted for the purpose did not fabricate a suitable
place of shelter. The monkey felt the reflection implied in the
admonition, and unable to restrain his indignation, jumped into the tree
and destroyed the residence of his innocent friend.
In my journeys on foot in various parts of this country I have
frequently seen the nests of the pendulous bird. On a tree not many
miles from Trincomalie on the eastern coast of Ceylon I once saw perhaps
thirty or forty nests hanging from onetree not twenty feet high: they
resembled large pears.
3912. tūkki niṉaittu nōkkip pēcu.
Speak after deliberation and circumspection.
3913. tūkki vaḷartta piḷḷaiyum tuṭaiyil vaittut taitta ilaiyum uruppaṭātu.
Achild brought up in the arms, and a leaf plate stitched whenspread on
the lap, will not be properly formed.
3914. tūkki niṟuttaṭā piṇakkāṭāy veṭṭukiṟēṉ.
Lift me up you fellow, I will cut them down and convert the placeinto a
burningground.
3915. tūkki viṉaicey.
Do a thing after due deliberation.
3916.tūkku uṇṭāṉāl nōkku uṇṭu.
Gravity inspires respect.
3917. tūṅkātavaṉē nīṅkātavaṉ.
The sleepless is the abiding one.
3918. tūṅkātavaṉukkuc cukam illai.
He who does not sleep is not healthy.
3919. tūṅkiṉavaṉatu kaṭākkuṭṭi, viḻittiruntavaṉatu ūṭṭuk kuṭṭi.
The weaned he-goat belongs to him who slept, the suckling to him who was
awake.
3920. tūṅkiṉavaṉ kaṉṟu kaṭākkaṉṟu.
The calf of him who slept is a bull calf.
3921. tūṅkiṉavaṉ cākiṟatillai, vīṅkiṉavaṉ piḻaikkiṟatillai.
A patient who sleeps well will not soon die, one whose body is swollen
will not live.
3922. tūṇṭiṟkāraṉukkuk kaṇ eṅkē mitappil allavō?
Where is the eye of the angler? on the float, is it not?
3923. tūmat tīyaip pārkkilum kāmat tī koṭitu.
The fire of lust is more fierce than a smoking fire.
3924. tūra iruntāl cēra uṟavu.
Distance promotes close friendship.
3925. tūrattup paccai kaṇṇukkuk kuḷircci.
Green in the distance is cooling to the eye.
3926. tūrattup pārvaikku malai maḻamaḻappu, kiṭṭappōṉāl kallum kaṟaṭum.
A mountain in the distance appears smooth, but as we approach it, the
surface becomes rugged.
3927. tūrattut taṇṇīr āpattukku utavātu.
Water at a distance is not available in an emergency.
3928. tūrap pōkavēṇṭumō kīraip pāttiyil kai vaikka?
Must I go away that you may lay your hands on the bed of greens ?
3929. tūrnta kiṇaṟṟait turavākkātē.
Do not empty a well that has been filled up.
3930. tūṟāṉa kuṭi nīṟākum.
A profligate family will be reduced to ashes.
3931. tūṟṟar eṉpōr col eḻuttu uṇarār.
The slanderous are ignorant of letters.
3932. tūṟṟit tiriyēl.
Do not go about slandering.
3933. tūṟṟum peṇṭīr kūṟṟu eṉat takum.
A reproachful wife may fitly be called Yama.
3934. teṇṭattukkup paṇamum tivacattiṟkuk kaṟiyum akappaṭum.
Money for fines, and greens for the anniversaries of the dead, are
readily available.
3935. teyva cittam iruntāl cettavaṉum eḻumpuvāṉ.
God being willing, even the dead may rise.
3936. teyvap pulavaṉukku nā uṇarum cittira ōṭāvikkuk kai uṇarum.
To a heaven-inspired poet, the tongue, to an artist, the hand are
skilful.
3937. teyvam tuṇaikkōḷ tēkam anittiyam.
Secure divine help,- the body is transient.
3938. teriyāt tiṇaiyē piriyāt tuṇai nī.
Ouņconscicous earth, thou art my inseparable help.
3939. teruḷā maṉatukku iruḷē illai.
An unenlightened mind has no sense of darkeness.
3940. teviṭṭāk kaṉi piḷḷai , teviṭṭāp pāṉam taṇṇīr.
A child is a fruit that does not nauseate, and water is a beveragethat
never cloys.
3941.teḷinta taṇṇīr nīr kuṭittīr cēṟṟaik kalakki viṭṭīr.
Having drunk the clear water, thou hast stirred the mud.
3942.teḷivuṟum paratēva tēcikaṉ.
The illustrious, holy, divine teacher.
3943. teḷḷiya tirumaṇi tiruṭṭukku navamaṇi.
In thieving he is a precious stone- one of the nine gems.
3944.teṟikka aṭitta taṭṭāṉaip pōlē.
Like the goldsmith who beats off the gold inpieces.
3945. teṟkattaik kuruviyai vaṭakkattaik kuruvi teṟṟi aḻaittatām cīkampaḻam tiṉṉap pōka.
A northern bird induced a southern bird to feed on Sigampalam- a fruit.
3946. teṟkē aṭitta kāṟṟut tirumpi aṭiyātā?
Will not the south wind blow again?
3947. teṉ kāci ācāram tirunelvēli upacāram.
The manners of Tenkasi, and the ceremoniousness of Tinnevelly.
3948. teṉṟal muṟṟip perum kāṟṟu āccutu.
The gentle southern breeze has increased to a gale.
3949. teṉṉamarattiṟ pāti eṉṉai vaḷarttāḷ pāvi .
I am half the height of a cocoanut tree, she who brought me up is a
sinner.
3950. teṉṉamarattilē tēḷ koṭṭap paṉaimarattilē neṟi kaṭṭiṉatu pōla.
As when the scorpion stung the cocoanut tree, the palmyra treehad a
glandular swelling.
3951. teṉṉālirāmaṉ pūṉai vaḷarttatu pōla.
As Tennaluraman reared acat.
3952. tēṅkāy tiṉṟavaṉ tiṉṉak kōmpai cūppiṉavaṉ teṇṭam iṟukkiṟatā ?
While he who ate the kernal of the cocoanut escapes with impunity, is he
who sucked the fibre to pay the fine?
3953. tēcaṅkaḷ tōṟum pāṣaikaḷ vēṟu.
As countries differ, their languages-differ also.
3954. tēṭappōṉa mūlikai kālaṭiyil akappaṭṭār pōla.
As if a medicinal root he had gone to seek, was obtained at his foot.
3955. tēṭit tiruviḷakku vai.
Prepare to place a sacred lamp.
3956. tēṭit tiṉṟavar teyvattōṭu ottavar.
He who earns his own bread is like god.
3957. tēṭip putaittut teruvil irakkiṟatā?
What! begging in the street, after having acquired wealth andburied it?
3958. tēṭiya poruḷ kālilē taṭṭiṉatupōla.
As the thing one is in search of hits the foot.
3959. tēṭiṉa pūṇṭu kālilē mitipaṭṭatu.
The plant one was looking for was being trodden under foot.
3960. tēṭuvār aṟṟa piṇam teruvōṭē.
No one being interested in its burial, the corpse lies in the road.
3961. tēmpaṭu paṉaiyiṉ tiraḷ paḻattoru virai.
A selected nut from a number of sweet palmirah fruit.
3962. tēyntālum cantaṉakkaṭṭai maṇam pōkātu.
Though worn by attrition, the sandal wood loses not its fragrance.
3963. tēyntu mūñcūṟu āṉīrō kompukaḷ utirttīrō?
Art thou worn out and become a musk-rat, hast thou cast thy horns?
3964. tērāc ceykai tīrāc cañcalam.
A thoughtless act occasions endless trouble.
3965. tērai mōrnta tēṅkāy pōl.
Like the cocoanut smelt by a toad-a blighted cocoanut.
3966. tērōṭē niṉṟu teruvōṭē alaikiṟāṉ.
He wanders along the street with the temple car.
3967. tērntavaṉ eṉpatu kūrntu aṟivataṉāl.
He is called intelligent because of his nice discrimination.
3968. tēvaṭiyāḷ teru koḷḷai pōkiṟatā ?
Is robbery committed in a street of harlots ?
3969. tēvaṭiyāḷ ciṅkārikku muṉṉē tēr ōṭit teruvil niṉṟatu.
Before the dancing girl had adorned herself, the car moved and came to a
stand on
the road.
3970. tēvaṭiyāḷ makaṉ tivacam ceytatupōla.
As the son of a harlot commemorated the death of his father.
3971. tēvaṭiyāḷ cettāl piṇam, tēvaṭiyāḷ tāy cettāl maṇam.
When a harlot dies the body is a mere corpse, when her mother died
funeral rites are observed.
3972. tēvaraik kāṭṭip pūtam paṇi koḷḷum.
Under pretence that gods require it, goblins exact service from men.
Goblins or ghosts of the kind referred to, are employed by magicians.
They are supposed to haunt grave yards, places of incremation, buried
treasure &c.Companies of them attendSiva, Ganesa &c.
3973. tēvarkaḷ paṇiviṭai ceyyu mēlavaṉ karttā.
He is the Lord whom the celestials serve.
3974. tēḷ koṭṭap pāmpukku mantirikkiṟatā?
When stung by a scorpion do you recite incantations as for a snakebite?
3975. tēḷ neruppil viḻuntāl eṭuttuviṭṭavaṉaik koṭṭum.
The scorpion, stings him who helps it out of the fire.
3976. tēṉil viḻunta ī pōlat tavikkiṟāṉ.
He flutters as a fly that has fallen, into honey.
3977. tēṉukku īyait tēṭi viṭuvār ār?
Who cares to supply flies for honey?
3978. tēṉum tiṉaimāvum tēvarukku arppitam.
Honey mixed with tinai flour is offered to the gods.
3979. tēṉum pālum pōl irukkavēṇṭum.
Live in harmony, as honey and milk.
3980.tēṉum pālum pōl iruntu kaḻuttai aṟuttāṉ.
Pretending friendship as sweet as honey and milk, he cut my throat.
3981. tēṉēpōlum centamiḻk kalvi.
Mellifluous classical Tamil.
3982. tēṉait toṭṭiyō nīrait toṭṭiyō?
Did you touch honey, or did you touch water?
3983. tēṉ uṇṭāṉāl ī tēṭivarum.
Where there is honey there are flies.
3984. tēṉ uḷḷa iṭattil ī moykkum.
Flies swarm where there is honey.
3985. tēṉ eṭuttavaṉukku oru coṭṭu , māmaṉ maṉaiyil iruntavaṉukku oru coṭṭu.
A cuff for him who steals honey, a cuff for him who lives in the house
of his father-in-law.
3986. tēṉ oḻukappēcit teruk kaṭakka vaḻiviṭuvāṉ.
He speaks to him mellifluously, and accompanies him across the street-in
order to get rid of him.
3987. tēṉ oḻukap pēcuvāṉ.
He speaks mellifluously.
3988. tēṉ kūṭṭilē kallaiviṭṭu eṟiyalāmā?
May you throw stones at a bee-hive?
3989. tēṉ toṭṭār kai nakkārō?
Will those who have touched honey not lick their fingers ?
3990. tēṉ toṭṭavaṉ puṟaṅkai nakkāmaṟ pōvāṉā?
When one has touched honey, will he not lick the back of his hand?
3991. tēṉ vārttu vaḷarttālum kāñciram teṅku ākumō?
Will the gall-nut become as sweet as acocoanut though watered with
honey?
3992. tai īṉāp pullum illai , māci īṉā maramum illai.
There is no grass that does notgrow in January, nor tree that does not
sprout in February.
3993. taitta vāyilum irukkat tāṇitta vāyilum irukka eṅkālē pōṉīr uppaṉārē?
Whilst one gate is blocked up, and the other defended by artillery, O
Uppanar howdid you find your way in?
3994. tai piṟantāl vaḻi piṟakkum.
If January come, roads come.
3995. taippukkut taippu maram piṭittāṟpōl.
Like holdingthe lath as each nailis fastened.
3996. taiyalum illāṉ maiyalum illāṉ.
One who has neither wife, nor desire to marry.
3997. taiyum māciyum vaiyakattu uṟaṅku.
In January and February sleep under thatch.
Because dew is then excessive.
3998. toṭṭāl tōḻaṉ viṭṭāl māṟṟāṉ.
When together friends, if separated enemies.
3999. toṭṭiliṟ piḷḷaikku naṭakkiṟa piḷḷai namaṉ.
A child that can walk, is as Yama to a child in the cradle.
4000. toṭṭiyap pēy cuṭukāṭu maṭṭum.
The demon of a wizard pursues oneto the burring ground.
4001. toṭṭilum āṭṭip piḷḷaiyum kiḷḷuvāḷ.
Sherocks the cradle and pinches the child.
4002. toṭṭiṟ paḻakkam cuṭukāṭu maṭṭum.
Habits contracted in the cradle cleave to one till he goes to the
burning ground.
4003. toṭṭuk kāṭṭāta vittai coṭṭup pōṭṭālum varātu.
Without a preceptor an art cannot be attained.
4004. toṇṭarkaḷ aṉpaṉ tuṇaikku niṟpavaṉ.
He is the friend of his servants who helps them.
4005. toṇṭeṉappaṭēl.
Be not called a slave.

Tamil proverbs translation by Percival
Part 3 (third 2000 proverbs)
4006. toṇṇūṟu kaṭaṉōṭē tuvaramparuppuk kāṟ paṇam.
With ninety debts, beans for a quarter of a fanam.
4007. toṇṇūṟu poṉṉōṭē tuvaramparuppu oru paṇam.
With ninety gold fanams, one for beans.
4008. tottukkut tottuc cāṭci tuvaramparuppukku mattē cāṭci.
A slave is a witness for a slave, a churn-staff is a witness for
beans-cytisus cajan.
4009. toḻutūṇ cuvaiyiṉ uḻutūṇ iṉitu.
The food earned by the plough is sweeter than that obtained by serving
others.
4010. toḻuvam pukunta āṭu puḻukkai iṭāmaṟ pōmā?
When sheep are penned, will not dung be found in the fold?
4011. toṉmai nāṭi naṉmai viṭātē.
In your zeal for old forms neglectnot what is really useful.
4012. toṉmai maṟavēl.
Forget not your former condition.
4013. toṉṉilam muḻutum tōṉṟiya kalvi.
Learning which is conspicuous to the whole world.
4014. tōṭṭakkāraṉ vāḻvu kāṟṟu aṭittāl pōyiṟṟu.
The prospects of a gardener are destroyed by a gale.
4015. tōṭṭattil antam.
It ends in the garden.
4016. tōṭṭattu nari kūṭṭattil varumā?
Will the jackal of the garden come into the assembly?
4017. tōṭṭam muccāṇ curaikkāy aṟucāṇ.
The garden is three spans square, the gourd in it is six spans long.
4018. tōṭṭam nilaittallavō teṉṉampiḷḷai vaikkavēṇṭum.
It is after laying out the garden plot, is it not, that cocoanut trees
are planted.
4019. tōṭṭipōl uḻaittāl turaipōlac cāppiṭalām.
If you work like a scavenger, you may eat like your master.
4020. tōṇi pōkum tuṟai kiṭakkum.
The ship goes, the port remains.
4021. tōṇṭak kuṟuṇi tūrkka mukkuṟuṇi.
One kuruni for digging, three for filling up.
4022. tōl irukkac cuḷai viḻuṅki.
He who can swallow the pulp while the peel remains intact.
4023. tōḻaṉāvatu tulaṅkiya kalvi.
Distinguished learning is a real companion.
4024. tōḻaṉōṭum ēḻaimai pēcēl.
Disclose not your defects even to a friend.
2025. tōḷil iruntu ceviyaik kaṭikkiṟatā ?
What! seated on the shoulder and biting the ear?
4026. tōṟpatu koṇṭu capai ēṟukiṟatā?
Do you enter the assembly when sure of defeat ?
4027. tōṟṟiḷa yāvum tōṟṟam aṟṟu oḻiyum.
All things that exist will vanish away.
4028. teḷavit tiriyēl.
Do not romp about.
4029. teḷavaiyiṉ maṉatukku ovvutal illai.
Nothing gives satisfaction to the goddess of misfortune.
4030. nakattālē kiḷḷuvataik kōṭari koṇṭu veṭṭukiṟatā?
Do you use a hatchet when the nails would suffice ?
4031. nakamum cataiyum pōlē vāḻukiṟāṉ.
United like the nails and the flesh.
4032. nakaraikkup pettai vaḻikāṭṭukiṟatō?
Is a pettai-a small fish a guide to a nagarai fish?
4033. nakaikku makiḻcci naṭpukku nañcu.
Merriment is the poison of friendship.
4034. nakaiccol tarutal pakaikku ētuvākum.
Reproachful words lead to enmity.
4033. nakaittu ikaḻvōṉai nāyeṉa niṉai.
Regard him as a dog who laughs you to scorn.
4036. nakkukiṟa nāykkuc cekku eṉṟum civaliṅkam eṉṟum teriyumā?
Does a dog addicted to licking, distinguish between an oil-press and a
Siva linga ?
The linga, the symbol of the Saiva worship, is anointed with oil.
4037. nakkukiṟapoḻutu nāvu eḻumpumā?
Does the tongue rise when licking ?
When eating the food of another reproachful language is not used.
4038. nakku uṇṭār nāvu eḻār.
Those who lick do not raise the tongue.
Those who have eaten the food of another man will not reproach him.
4039. naccu maram āṉālum naṭṭavarkaḷ veṭṭuvārkaḷā?
Though a poisonous tree, will those who planted it cut it down?
4040. nañcukkuḷ iruntālum nākamaṇi māṇikkamē, kuppaikkuḷ iruntālum kuṉṟimaṇi
kuṉṟimaṇiyē.
Though associated with poison, the stone on a cobra's head is a gem;
though it may be found in a dunghill, a kunrimani is still the same.
4041. nañcu nāṟkalam vēṇṭumā?
Are four kalams of poison required?
4042. naṭakka aṟiyātavaṉukku naṭuvīti kātavaḻi.
Toa person unaccustomed to walk, the middle of his house is ten miles
off.
4043. naṭakkiṟatu naṭakkaṭṭum teyvam irukkiṟatu.
Let things take their course ; there is a God.
4044. naṭakkum kāl tavaṟuvatilum nā tavaṟutal keṭṭatu.
A slip of the tongue is worse than that of the foot.
4045. naṭanta piḷḷai tavaṟutām tāyār ceyta tavattāl.
It is said to be owing to the penance of the mother that the child that
could walk has begun to crawl.
4046. naṭantavaṉ kālilē cītēvi, iruntavaṉ kālilē mūtēvi.
The goddess of fortune dwells in the feet of the industrious, the
goddess of misfortune dwells in the feet of the sluggard.
The goddess of prosperity or fortune, and the goddess of adversity or
misfortune, two sisters, mentioned in this proverb, exercise a
mysterious influence over the minds of the vulgar. These remarkable
beings are said to have been produced when the celestials, in search of
ambrosia, churned the milky ocean. The opinion expressed in the proverb
is also contained in a stanza of Nitinerivillakkam a poem on moral
subjects, justly admired by the learned, for the terseness and beauty of
its composition, as well as for the general purity of its moral
sentiments. The poet says:-
“When the goddess of prosperity finds that her favours are not
appreciated, she introduces her elder sister, the goddess of adversity,
to the sluggard, and then takes her departure."
The goddess of fortune, whose presence is a guarantee for happiness
andprosperity, is said to dwell in the face of a horse of superior
caste, -with the wise and good,-in all fragrant flowers,-in the tree of
paradise that bestows whatever it's votaries may desire,-in the beauty
ofa good and obedient wife, -in the ocean,-in the portal of a house
where a marriage is being celebrated,-in a well governed country,-in
grain in brilliant lamps and flaming torches,-in the words of the
great,-with truth-speaking men,-in the arrows of Cupid, -and in vessels
of milk.
The goddess of misfortune is said to have her habitation with the
glutton.-the irascible, - with liars and other abandoned characters, -
with those clothed in rags,-with vicious women,-in a flock of sheep, and
in the face of the dead.
4047. naṭantāl nāṭu ellām uṟavu, paṭuttāl pāyum pakai.
The whole country is friendly to one who is active, his own mat is at
enmity with the sluggard.
4048. naṭapaṭi uṇṭāṉāl mitiyaṭi poṉṉālē.
If allowance for a journey is provided, gold sandals will be used.
4049. naṭavukkut teḷi nālattoṉṟu.
The yield of rice is a fourth part of that planted.
4050.naṭu uḻavilē nantai teṟittatu pōla.
Like the snapping of the yoke tie when the plough has done half its
work.
4051. naṭu ūril naccumaram paḻuttu eṉṉa?
Of what use is the ripening of the fruit of a poisonous tree in the
middle of a village ?
4052. naṭukkaṭaṟ pōṉālum maṟuppaṭāmal varakkaṭavīr.
Though you go to the middle of the sea, return uninjured.
4053. naṭutterup piccaikku nāṇayam pārkkalāmā?
Can one retain self-respect who receives alms in the middle of the road?
4054. naṭuttaramāṉavaruṭaiya tāṅkal poṉṉiṉ piḷavu pōlap paṟṟavaittāl māṟum.
A fracture in gold vanishes when exposed to the fire; in like manner the
anger of the good passes away.
4055. naṭuvu nilaimai uṭaiyavarkkuc ceyta upakāram koñcakkālattiṟkuniṟkum; atupōla,
tāmaraiyil viḻunta maḻaittuḷi niṟkāmal maṟaiyum.
A benefit conferred on the indifferent is remembered for a short time,
so a drop of rain on a lotus leaf vanishes soon.
4056. naṭai ciṟitākil nāḷ ēṟum, paṭai ciṟitākiṟ payam ēṟum.
If one's pace be slow he will be long on the way, if an army be small,
its anxiety will be great.
4057. naṣṭattukkup palar nayattukku oruvaṉō?
Is one to have the gain, and many to share the loss ?
4058. naṣṭattukku oruvaṉ nayattukku oruvaṉ.
One loses, one gains.
4059. naṭṭāṟṟilē kaiviṭukiṟatu naṉmaiyā?
Is it kind to abandon one in the middle of a river?
4060. naṭṭuvaṉ piḷḷaikku muṭṭaṭikkat teriyātā?
Does not the child of a drummer know how to drum?
4061. naṭṭuvaṉ piḷḷaikkuk koṭṭik kāṭṭavēṇṭumā?
Does the child of a drummer require a preceptor?
4062. naṇṭuk kuṭuvaiyai naṭutteruvil uṭaittatu pōla.
Like breaking in the middle of the street a pot containing crabs.
4063. naṇṭu koḻuttāl vaḷaiyil irātu.
The crab will not remain in its hole when it becomes fat.
4064. naṇṭaic cuṭṭu nariyaik kāval vaittatu pōla.
Like setting a jackal to watch a roasted crab.
4065. naṇporuḷ koṭuttu, naṉṟāy ōtu.
Pay the teachers fee, and learn well.
4066. natta vāḻaiyilai nittam kāṟpaṇam.
A village plantain leaf always costs a quarter fanam.
4067. nattai vayiṟṟil muttup piṟantatu.
Pearls are produced in the bellyof an oyster or snail.
4068. nanta vaṉattu āṇṭikkum muyal vēṭṭaikkum ettaṉai tūram.
How distant are the occupations of the mendicant of the grove, and of
the hare - hunter.
4069. napāp attaṉai ēḻai puli attaṉai cātu.
Poor as a Nabob, and gentle as a tiger.
4070. namatu talaimayir avaṉ kaiyil akappaṭṭukkoṇṭatu.
The hair of the head is seized by his hand.
4071. namaṉukku nālu piḷḷai koṭuttālum, uṟṟārukku oru piḷḷaikoṭukkamāṭṭāṉ.
Though he may give four children to Yama, he will not give one to his
relatives.
4072. namaṉ eṭuttukkoṇṭu pōkum poḻutu naḻuvi viḻuntavaṉ.
He slipped and fell when Yama was carrying himoff.
4073. namaṉ aṟiyāta uyirum nārai aṟiyāta kuḷamum uṇṭō?
Is there a soul that Yama knows not, is there a tank unknown to the
crane?
4074. nampa vaittuk kaḻuttu aṟukkalāmā?
May you cut a man's throat after ingratiating yourself in hisconfidence?
4075. nampiṉa pērai naṭu āṟṟil viṭalāmā?
May you leave in the middle of the river, those who have confided
themselves to your care ?
4076.nari ūraiviṭṭup puli ūrukkup pōṉēṉ puli ūrum nari ūrāyiṟṟu.
Leaving the region of jackals I went to that of the tigers, and that
became a region of jackals.
4077. nari oru cālukku uḻappōṉatu.
The jackalis gone to plough.
4078. nari kūppiṭṭuk kaṭal muṭṭumā?
Will the howling of the fox reach the sea ?
4079. nari kūppiṭu kaṭal muṭṭippōkum.
The howling of the jackal will reach the ocean.
4080. nari koḻuttāl vaḷaiyil irātu.
If the jackal becomes fat it will not remain in its hole.
4081. nari koḻuttu eṉṉa kāñciram paḻuttu eṉṉa ?
What if the jackal becomes fat, or, the gall-nut ripen?
4082. narikkuṭṭikku ūḷai paḻakka vēṇṭumā?
Is the young jackal to be trained to howl?
4083. narikkup periyataṉam koṭuttāl kiṭaikku iraṇṭu āṭu kēṭkum.
If the jackal gains the mastery, he will demand two sheep from every
flock.
4084. nari nālu kāl tiruṭaṉ iṭaiyaṉ iraṇṭu kāl tiruṭaṉ.
The jackal is a quadraped thief, the shepherd is a biped thief.
4085. nari muṉṉē naṇṭu karaṇam pōṭṭatu pōla.
As the crab made somersaults before a jackal.
4086. nariyiṉ kaiyilē kuṭal kaḻuvak koṭukkiṟatā ?
Are viscera to be given into the hands of a jackal to be washed ?
4087. narivālaik koṇṭu kaṭal āḻam pārkkiṟatu pōla.
Like measuring the depth of the ocean with the tail of a jackal.
4088. narai tirai illai, namaṉum aṅku illai.
He is not grey nor wrinkled ; Yama is not there.
4089. nal iṇakkamallatu allaṟ paṭuttum.
Evil communications will bring distress.
4090. nal iṉattil naṭpu valitu.
Friendship is stronger than close relationship.
4091. nalla uyir nāṟpatu nāḷ irukkum.
A good man's life may continue forty days.
4092. nalla eḻuttu naṭuvē irukkak kōṇal eḻuttuk kuṟukkē pōṭṭatu eṉṉa?
A good letter-destiny-being in the middle, how were the crooked letters
put in ?
4093. nalla amaiccu illāta aracu viḻi iṉṟi vaḻic celvōṉ pōlām.
A king without a good counsellor, is like a wayfaring man who is blind.
4094. nallatu keṭṭāl nāykkum vaḻaṅkātu.
When the good becomes bad, even a dog will not use it.
4095. nallatukku oru pollātatu, pollāttukku oru nallatu.
A good husband may have a bad wife, and a bad husband may have a good
wife.
4096. nalla kuṭikku nālattoru paṅkāḷi.
One who holds a fourth share in a prosperous family.
4097. nallatu ceytu naṭuvaḻiyē pōṉāl pollātu pōkiṟavaḻiyē pōkiṟatu.
If you do good and walk in the middle of the road, the evil will find
its own way.
4098. nallatu nāṟkalam, ūttai oṉpatu kalam.
Of the good only four kalams, of the bad nine kalams.
4099. nalla nāḷaiyil nāḻip pāl kaṟavātatu kaṉṟu cettuk kalappāl kaṟakkumō ?
In its best days the cow gave scarcely a measure of milk, will it yield
a kalam after its calf is dead?
This proverb may refer to the fact that milch, cows in this part of
India and in Ceylon refuse to give their milk in the absence of the
calf. The cow when going to a neighbouring house to be milked, is
accompanied by its calf which is often muzzled like a dog. When the
milkman is about to draw the milk he allows the calf to suck for a few
moments and then tying it to the fore leg of the mother, he draws the
milk, while she stands quiet licking her offspring. Cows do sometimes
allow their milk to be taken when the calf is absent but it not
infrequently happens that the cow refuses to give her milk, and the calf
is fetched from the homestead to encourage the mother.
If a calf die, its skin may be dried and stuffed. The cow deceived by
the device licks the effigy of her calf and yields her milk.
4100. nalla niṉaivai aṉucarittalē keṭṭa niṉaivai nīkkal.
Entertaining good thoughts, is in fact leaving evil thoughts.
4101. nalla pāmpu āṭiyatu kaṇṭu nākappūcci āṭiyatupōla.
As the earth-worm imitated the graceful movements of the cobra.
4102. nalla maram naccuk kaṉiyait tarātu, naccu marattilē nalla kaṉiyum varātu.
A good tree yields not poisonous fruit, nor a poisonous tree good fruit.
4103. nalla marattil muḷaitta pulluruvipōla.
Like a mistletoe growing on a good tree.
4104. nalla māṭu āṉāl uḷḷūril vilai ākātā?
If the cow be a good one, will it not find a purchaser in its own
village?
4105. nalla māṭṭiṟku oru aṭi , nalla peṇṇukku oru col.
A good bullock requires but one blow, and a good woman only one word.
4106. nallavar kaṇṇil nākam paṭṭālum kollār.
Though it is before them the virtuous will not kill even a cobra.
4107. nallavaṉ eṉṟu peyar eṭukka nāṭ cellum.
Time must elapse before one can get a good name.
4108. nallavarkaḷ oru nāḷ ceyta upakārattai maṟavār; atupōla, paṉai oru nāḷ vitaittut
taṇṇīr viṭṭavaṉukku palaṉ koṭukkum.
The good never forget a benefit; in like manner a palmyrah tree yields
its produce to him who planted and watered it.
4109. nallavaṉ oruvaṉ naṭuvē niṉṟāl aṟāta vaḻakkum aṟum.
If there be but one good man present in a suit, the greatest
difficulties will be solved.
4110. nalla vārttai colli nāṭiyait tāṅkiṉāṉ.
He spoke conciliatory words, and touched my chin cringingly.
4111. nalla iḷaṅkaṉṟē tuḷḷātē.
My good young-calf, don’t be frisky.
4112. nallaṟam uḷḷatu illaṟam.
Domestic virtue is excellent.
4113. nalla vēlaikkāraṉ āṟṟōṭē pōkiṟāṉ.
He is a good servant, but the river carries him away.
4114. nalla uṭalukku iḷaippāṟṟi koṭāviṭṭālum nāviṟkumkoṭu.
Though you may not give rest to your body, give rest to your tongue.
4115. nallavarkaḷ caṅkāttam nalla maṇalil viḻunta nīrpōla utavum.
The friendship of the good will prove useful like water falling on good
soil.
4116. nallāraik kaṇṭāl nāypōla, pollāraik kaṇṭāl pūṉaipōla.
If he sees the good, a dog, if he sees the wicked, a cat.
4117. nallārai nāvu aḻiyap pēciṉāl pallālē patakkup puḻuccoriyum.
If one abuses his tongue by slandering the virtuous, maggots will drop
from his teeth.
4118. nallārum nalla pāmpaip pōlat taṅkaḷ valimaiyai aṭakkimaṟaintiruppārcila vēḷai.
The good, like the cobra, sometimes restrain their power and conceal
themselves.
4119. nallārai nāvil urai, poṉṉaik kallil urai.
Test the good by the words of the tongue, andgold on a touch-stone.
4120. nallār pollārai naṭakkaiyāl aṟiyalām.
The good and the bad may be known by their conduct.
4121. nallōr oruvarukkup peyyum maḻai ellārukkum ām.
The rain that falls on account of one virtuous person, is beneficial to
all.
4122. nallōr naṭakkai tīyōrukkut tikil.
The acts of the virtuous are a terror to the wicked.
4123. nallōṉ eṉa vaḷar, nāṭkaḷ pārēl.
Grow up virtuous, observe not days.
4124. naṟkuṇamē nalla āsti.
A good disposition is the best treasure.
4125. naṟpeṇṭīr nallataik kaṇṭāl namatu nāyakaṉukku eṉpār.
When a faithful wife finds a good thing, she will keep it for her
husband.
4126. naṉaittuc cumakkalāmā?
Would you wet your burden?
4127. naṉainta kiḻavaṉ vantāl ularnta viṟakukkuc cētam.
If the old man arrive wet, there will be a consumption of dry fire wood.
4128. naṉmai ceytār naṉmai peṟuvār , tīmai ceytār tīmai peṟuvār.
Those who do good, obtain good; and those who do evil receive evil.
4129. naṉmai ceyyat tīmai viḷaiyātu.
Evil will not spring from well-doing.
4130. naṉmaiyāṉataik keṭuttāl naṣṭattilum naṣṭam.
It is the greatest loss to destroy that which is good.
4131. naṉmaiyum tīmaiyum immaiyilē teriyum.
Good and evil are apparent in the present state.
4132. naṉmaiyaip perukkit tīmaiyaik kuṟaittal naṉṉeṟi.
To promote good and to diminish evil is the right way.
4133. naṉmai kaṭaippiṭi.
Persevere in that which is good.
4134. naṉṟāy iruntatām nallicuṭṭa paṇikāram.
The cakes prepared by Nalli are said to be excellent.
4135. naṉṟi maṟavēl.
Forget not a benefit.
4136. naṉṟi ceyta kīrippiḷḷaiyaik koṉṟa katai pōla.
Like the story of killing a mungoose that had done well.
The tale is-a mungoose seeing a deadly snake approach a sleeping infant,
killed it. When the mother returned from the well she saw blood on the
animal, and imagining that it had bitten her child she killed it.
4137. nā acaiya nāṭu acaiyum.
When the tongue moves, the whole country moves.
The allusion is to a despotic ruler whose word is law.
4138. nā eṉṉum aṭcaram nātaṉ iruppiṭam.
The letter na is the seat of God.
4139. nākkukku elumpu illai eppaṭip puraṭṭiṉālum appaṭip puraḷum.
The tongue having no bone will turn any way.
4140. nākkup puraṭṭar pōkkup pukalvar.
Promise-breakers make excuses.
4141. nāṅkaḷum kaṅkaṇam kaṭṭiṉatu uṇṭu kaḻuttukkuk kaṅkaṇamkaṭṭiṉatu illai.
We too tied coloured cords about our arms, but not a halter on tho neck.
4142. nācciyārum oṉṟaippaṟṟi vārkkiṟāḷ nāṉum oṉṟaippaṟṟik kuṭikkiṟēṉ.
The mistress pours it out with one design, and I drink it with another.
4143. nācciyāraik kāṇāta iṭattilē muṟumuṟuppatupōla.
Like murmuring in the absence of the mistress.
4144. nāṭiya varam ellām nalkum nāyakaṉ.
A master who bestows all desired blessings.
4145. nāṭu aṟinta pārppāṉukkup pūṇūl vēṇṭumō?
Is a sacred thread necessary for a brahman, who is known throughout the
country?
4146. nāṭu eṅkum vāḻantāl kēṭu oṉṟum illai.
If the whole country prospers, no evil will happen.
4147. nāṭu ērppaṉa cey.
Do what is agreeable to the community.
4148. nāṭu kāṭāyiṟṟu kāṭu kaḻaṉi āyiṟṟu.
The country has become a jungle, and the jungle has become a fruitful
field.
4149. nāṭ ceṉṟa koṭai naṭaikūli ākum.
A delayed gift becomes the hire for walking to receive it.
4150. nāṭṭāḷ peṟṟa kuṭṭi nākarīkam pēca valla kuṭṭi.
The child of the peasant is able to speak elegantly.
4151. nāṭṭāḷukku oru nīṭṭāḷō?
Does a boor require a page?
4152. nāṭṭukku nalla turai vantālum tōṭṭikkup pul cumai pōkātu.
Though the country has a good ruler, the scavenger is not relieve of his
burden of grass.
4153. nāṭṭukku oru maḻai namakku iraṇṭu maḻai.
The country has received one downpour of rain, we have received two
i.e., too much.
4154. nāṇamum illai māṉamum illai.
No shame, no sense of honour.
4155. nāṇam illāta vāttikku nālu tikkum kūtti.
Concubines on all sides to a shameless teacher.
4156. nāṇam illāta kūttikku nālu tikkum vācal.
A shameless harlot has entrances on four sides.
4157. nāṇam illāta peṇ nakaippukku iṭam vaippāḷ.
An impudent woman gives occasion to be laughed at.
4158. nāṇum kāl kōṇum naṭakkum kāl iṭaṟum.
The foot of diffidence deviates, that of activity stumbles.
4159. nātamum kītamum ottiruppatu pōla, vētamum pōtamum ottirukka vēṇṭum.
As a melody and a song are in harmony, so must instruction be in harmony
with the Veda.
4160. nām oruvarukkuk koṭuttāl namakku oruvar koṭuppār.
If we give to others, some one will give to us.
4161. nām oṉṟu niṉaittāl teyvam oṉṟu niṉaikkiṟatu.
When we think of one thing, the deity designs another.
4162. nāy aṟiyumā oru cantip pāṉai ?
Does a dog know which are sacred vessels?
4163. nāyiṉ pīyai mitippāṉ ēṉ, nalla taṇṇīr vārttuk kaḻuvuvāṉ ēṉ?
Why tread on the dung of a dog, and waste good water to wash it off?
4164. nāyum kaḷiccaṭṭiyum pōla.
Like a dog with a chatty of thick gruel.
4165. nāyum cari nāviyum cari uṉakku.
A dog and a civet cat are both alike to you.
4166. nāyum taṉ nilattukku rājā.
Even a dog is king in his own place.
4167. nāyum pūṉaiyum pōla.
Like dogs and cats.
4168. nāyum vaḷarttu narakalum vāruvāṉēṉ?
Why keep a dog and clean up its filth?
4169. nāyēṉ col ampalattukku ēṟumō?
I am but a dog, will my word reach the assembly?
4170. nāyai aṭippāṉ ēṉ pal iḻivu pārppāṉ ēṉ?
Why beat a dog, why make it grin?
4171. nāyai aṭippāṉēṉ pīyaic cumappāṉēṉ?
Why boat a dog and carry away his filth?
4172. nāyai ēva, nāy vālai ēvukiṟatu.
When you command a dog, he commands his tail.
4173. nāyaik kaṇṭāl kallaik kāṇōm, kallaik kaṇṭāl nāyai kāṇōm.
If we see a dog, there is no stone, and if we see a stone, there is no
dog.
4174. nāyaik kuḷippāṭṭi naṭuvīṭṭil vaittāl vālaik kuḻaittukkoṇṭu pī tiṉṉap
pōkum.
If you wash a dog and place him in the middle of the house, he will wag
his tail and go out to eat filth.
4175. nāyaik koñciṉāl vāyai nakkum.
If you caress a dog, he will lick your mouth.
4176. nāyaik koḻukkaṭṭaiyāl eṟintatu pōla.
Like pelting a dog with cakes.
4177. nāy irukkiṟa iṭattil caṇṭai uṇṭu.
Where there are dogs, there is quarreling.
4178. nāy oru ciṟu elumpukkuc cantōṣikkum; atu pōla, ciṟiyōr coṟpa kāriyattai
muṭittālum cantōṣam aṭaivār.
A dog is pleased with a bone, in like manner the low are pleased with
their own little acts.
4179. nāy kaṭittataṟkum ceruppāl aṭittataṟkum cari.
The biting of a dog and the slippering of the wound to effect a cure are
alike, painful.
4180. nāy kulaittu nattam pāḻ ākumā?
Will the village be ruined by the barking of a dog?
4181. nāy kulaittu viṭiyumā, kōḻi kūppiṭṭu viṭiyumā?
Does the day dawn because the dog barks, or because the cock crows?
4182. nāy keṭṭa kēṭṭukku māmarattu niḻal, atu keṭṭa kēṭṭukkup puḷiyiṭṭa kaṟi.
Mean as the dog is, he has the shade of a mango tree, his curry is
flavoured with acid.
4183. nāy keṭṭa kēṭṭukkut tēṅkāyppālum cōṟumā?
Is a miserable dog to be fed with cocoanut milk and rice?
4184. nāy koṇṭupōṉa pāṉaiyai ār koṇṭupōṉāl eṉṉa?
What matters it who takes away the vessel that the dog had carried off?
4185. nāy kōvilukku pōvāṉ ēṉ kōyil kāttavaṉ teṇṭam iruppāṉ ēṉ?
Why should a dog go to a temple, or why the keeper of a temple pay a
fine?
4186. nāykkuk kaṭivāḷam pūṭṭiṉāṟpōla.
Like putting a bridle in the mouth of a dog.
4187. nāykkut teriyumā kokkup piṭikka?
Does a dog know how to catch a crane?
4188. nāykkut teriyumō tōl tēṅkāy ?
Does a dog appreciate an unhusked cocoanut ?
4189. nāykku naṟuney iṇaṅkātu.
Good cow ghee is not agreeable to a dog's stomach.
4190. nāykku irumpukkaṭaiyil aluval eṉṉa?
What business has a dog in an iron bazaar?
4191. nāykku muḻut tēṅkāy tākkumā?
Will a whole cocoanut suit a dog?
4192. nāykku vēlaiyum illai, irukka nēramum illai.
A dog has nothing to do, and no time to rest.
4193. nāykku mūttāl tāykkum īyāḷ.
If she becomes older than a dog, she will not be kind even to her
mother.
4194. nāykku ēṉ tēṅkāy, naṭuvīṭṭukkuḷ pōṭṭu uruṭṭavō?
Why give a whole cocoanut to a dog, that he may roll it about the house?
4195. nāykkup peyar muttumālai, ataṟku ākkip paṭaikkiṟatu varakantaviṭu.
The name of the dog is garland, its food is bran.
4196. nāy cantaikkup pōṉatu pōla.
As a dog went to the market.
4197. nāy camuttirattilē pōṉālum nakkut taṇṇīr.
Although a dog may go to the sea, the water must be lapped.
4198. nāy ciṅkattukkup paṭṭam kaṭṭumā?
Can a dog invest a lion with a title?
4199. nāy nakkic camuttiram kuṟaiyumā?
Will the ocean be diminished by the lapping of a dog?
4200. nāy paṭṭa pāṭu taṭikkompukkut teriyumā?
Are the sufferings of a dog known to the stick, with which he was
beaten?
4201. nāy vāyppaṭṭa tēṉ nallatu ākumā?
Is the honey, defiled by a dog, fit for use?
4202. nāy vālilē tēṉ vaittāl ārukkuk kūṭum?
Who will benefit by the honey placed on the tail of a dog?
4203. nāy vācalaik kāttu eṉṉa , kai illātavaṉ paṇakkāraṉaik kāttu eṉṉa?
What avails the waiting of a dog at the door, or the expectations of one
who, having no hands, waits on the rich?
4204. nāy vālaik kuṇakku eṭukkalāmā?
Can you change the shape of a dog's tail?
4205. nāy vālaip paṟṟi āṟṟil iṟaṅkalāmā?
May you descend into a river holding on by a dog's tail?
4206. nāy vāḻntu eṉṉa, pūṉai tāli aṟuttu eṉṉa?
What though a dog prosper, or a cat be bereft of her tali?
4207. nāy vēṣam pōṭṭāl kulaikkavēṇṭum.
If you assume the guise of a dog, you must bark.
4208. nār aṟṟāṟ kūṭum narampu aṟṟāṟ kūṭumā?
If a fibre snap it may be united, if a tendon break can it be united?
4209. nālām talaimuṟaiyaip pārttāl nāvitaṉum ciṟṟappaṉāvāṉ.
If relationship be traced to the fourth generation, even a barber may
become an uncle.
4210. nālāvatu peṇ nātāṅki muḷaikkum tikku illai.
A fourth born girl will not afford means even to procure a staple for a
bolt.
4211. nālu āṟu kūṭi oru pālāṟu āyiṟṟu.
If four rivers unite, the stream will be equal to the Palar.
4212. nālu piḷḷai peṟṟavaḷukku naṭut teruvilē cōṟu.
A woman who has borne four children, eats her rice in the middle of the
street.
4213. nālu pēr kūṭiṉatu capai.
The meeting of four persons is an assembly.
4214. nālum iraṇṭum collukku uṟuti, ālum vēlum pallukku uṟuti.
Naladiyar and the distichs of Valluvar are terse in construction, the
twigs of the banian tree and the Acacia are good for the teeth.
The tender fibre of the banian and Acacia are said to cure a gumboil,
and therefore they are used for cleaning the teeth.
4215. nālvarō tēvarō?
Are they the four, or are they celestials?
4216. nālvar vākkut tēvar vākku.
The testimony of four persons has the authority of a divine oracle.
4217. nāvil piṟakkum naṉmaiyum tīmaiyum.
The tongue produces good and evil.
4218. nāvukku icaintāl pāvukku icaiyum.
If agreeable to the tongue, it will be metrical.
4219. nāḻi arici cōṟu uṇṭavaṉ namaṉukku uyir koṭāṉ.
He that can eat a measure of rice may defy even the regent of the dead.
4220. nāḻi nellukku oru puṭaivai viṟṟālum nāyiṉ arai niruvāṇam.
Though a cloth is sold for a measure of rice, the dog goes naked.
4221. nāḻi paṇam koṭuttālum mūḷip paṭṭam pōkātu.
Though one give a measure of fanams, his ill fame will not be removed.
4222. nāḻi mukavātu nāṉāḻi.
One measure cannot contain four measures.
4223. nāḷ āṟṟukiṟatu nallār āṟṟār.
Time effects that which the virtuous cannot achieve.
4224. nāḷaikkut tiṉkiṟa palāppaḻattilum iṉṟaikkut tiṉkiṟa kaḷāppaḻam nallatu.
Better is the kala berry eaten to day, than the jack fruit in prospect
for to-morrow.
4225. nāṟat tūṟṟal narikkuk koṇṭāṭṭam.
The jackal is pleased with drizzling rain.
4226. nāṟal mīṉaip pūṉaip pārppatu pōla.
As the cat looks at the fish which has become putrid.
4227. nāṟal cāṇiyai mitippāṉ ēṉ nalla taṇṇīr vārttuk kaḻuvuvāṉ ēṉ?
Why tread on filth and waste good water in washing it off?
4228. nāṟaṟ cōṟṟukkup patam pārppāṉ ēṉ?
Why inquire if the rice is properly boiled, seeing that it is unfit for
food?
4229. nāṟaṟ caṭalam nalam illā maṭpāṇṭam.
A fetid body, a useless earthen vessel.
4230. nāṟkalak kūḻukku nāṉē atikāri.
I have the command of four kalams of gruel.
4231. nāṟpatu ceṉṟāl nāykkuc cari.
Like a dog, if over forty.
4232. nāṉilam taṉṉil nāyakam kalvi.
Learning is a gem when compared to the four kinds of soil.
They are the hilly,-forest,-agricultural and maritime tracts of a
country.
4233. nāṉum naraittu naraimaṇṭai āṉēṉ kāṭu naṭakkak kaṇṭatu putiṉam.
I have lived long and become grey-headed, but I have not seen a moving
jungle.
4234. nāṉum aṟiyēṉ avaḷum poy collāḷ.
I indeed do not know; nor will she tell a lie.
4235. nāṉ iṭṭa maruntum pōka oṭṭātu naṉṉāri vērum
cāka oṭṭātu.
The medicine I gave, as a charm, will not allow him to go; the
sarasparilla, aningredient, will not suffer him to die.
The proverb relates to charms, in which, as in magical arts generally,
the Hindus in every part of India and Ceylon, have great faith. Servants
have recourse to thisdevice, and lovers also, for the purpose of
influencing the affections and will of those whose favour or compliance
they desiderate.
The usual method, as indicated in the proverb, is to administer the food
of the party the ingredients that are fitted for the purpose
contemplated; of course the preparation is surreptitiously added to the
food of which it is known the individual is about to partake.
A gentleman of my acquaintance recently dismissed all his servant and
took into his service a person in whom he appeared to place the greatest
confidence. The neighbours and the discarded servant believe that the
gentleman acted under a charm.
Some of the things used for certain purposes as charms, may not be
mentioned here. The brain of a male child &c., are considered very(?)
potent.
Professional magicians are employed when stolen property or buried
treasure is sought. The magician uses a black preparation, a small
quantity of which is placed on a betel leaf and put into the hands of an
attendent boy, who is directed to look steadily at it, aided by the
light of a lamp, while the magician invokes the presence and aid of
certain deities. When the boy announces a phenomenon, say a tree, monkey
or a dog or a goblin, he is told to do obeisance to(?) to encourage
further discoveries. The earth may cleave as under(?) and reveal the
thing wanted, or a scene, including a house and certain persons going
and coming, water &c., may appear, when the boy proceeds to describe the
objects before him, as an earthen pot, or bangle &e., &c., as the case
may be.
To discover a thief among suspected persons sometimes dry grain as rice
may be given to be eaten. The person whoso mouth secretes no moisture
for mastication is supposed to be the thief.
4236. nāṉ oṉṟai eṇṇa viti oṉṟai eṇṇiṟṟu.
While I expectedone thing, destiny ordained another.
4237. nāṉ naṭṭēṉ nātaṉ payir ākkiṉāṉ.
I planted; god caused it grow.
4238. nāṉ piṭitta muyalukku mūṉṟu kāl.
The hare that I caught had three legs.
4239. nicam oṉṟu pala tīṅku nīkkum.
One thing ascertained will remove many evils.
4240. nicām alli taṇṭil nicārkkāraṉaik kaṇṭatu uṇṭā?
Was any trousered sepoy seen in the army of Nizam Aly?
4241. niṣṭūraṉ kaṇṇait teyvam keṭukkum, nītimāṉ kaṇṇaip paritāṉam keṭukkum.
God will destroy the eyes of an oppressor, and a bribe will destroy the
eye of an upright man.
4242. nittam cāvārkku aḻuvār uṇṭā ?
Do any weep when deaths are of daily occurrence ?
4243. nittam pōṉāl muṟṟam calikkum.
If you continually go thither, even the court-yard will be weary of you.
4244. nittiyak kaṇṭam tīrkka āyucu.
Constant trouble, long life.
4245. nittiyam kiṭaikkumā amāvācaic cōṟu ?
May new moon fare be expected daily ?
The proverb refers to the ceremonies which are performed, at the period
of the new moon, in honour of deceased ancestors. On these occasions the
household eat only once during the day, but the food is of a superior
kind and prepared with great care in cooking utensils that are kept for
sacred purposes. If a brahman be present he offers oblations of sesamum
grain and water to the manes of the dead, naming each in order as far
back as the third generation. In some parts of India ceremonies supposed
to affect the state of the dead are performed no less than ninety-six
times every year. The ceremonies of the Ancient Romans of like kind are
hinted at by Virgil and Horace as is known to the classical student.
4246. nittirai catturu.
Sleep is an enemy.
4247. nittirai cukam aṟiyātu.
Sleep is unconscious of enjoyment.
4248. nintaṉai collēl, nīti kaippiṭi.
Speak no reproachful words, do justice.
4249. nimiṣanēram niṟkum iṉpam ciṟṟiṉpam.
Momentary pleasure is inferior pleasure.
4250. nimiṣa nēram nīṭiya iṉpam.
A pleasure that lasts but a moment.
4251. nimittam pārkkiṟavaṉ iraṇṭakakkāri makaṉ, poruttam pārkkiṟavaṉ pollāṅkaṉ
puttiraṉ.
He who consults omens is the son of a deceitful woman, he who enquires
into the fitness of persons for marriage is the son of a vicious man.
4252. nimirntu pōṭṭatu eṉṉa kuṉintu eṭuttatu eṉṉa?
What is lost by an erect posture, and what gained by stooping?
4253. nimaip poḻutēṉum illai nīca uṭal.
The vile body will not endure even for the twinkling of an eye.
4254. niruvāṇa tēcattil nīrccīlai kaṭṭiṉavaṉ paittiyakāraṉ.
It were an act of folly to wear clothes in a country where all go naked.
4255. nilattil eḻunta pūṇṭu nilattil maṭiya vēṇṭum.
Plants decay on the ground from which they sprung.
4256. nilattiṟkut takunta kaṉiyum kulattiṟkut takunta kuṇamum.
Fruit appropriate to the soil, and quality agreeable, to one's rank.
4257. nilam kaṭakkap pāyalāmā?
Can you clear the earth at a leap ?
4258. nilavukku oḷittup paratēcam pōṉatu pōla.
Like going to another country to escape from moonlight.
4259. nilai illāṉ vārttai nīr mēl eḻuttu.
The words of the unstable are letters on water.
4260. nilaiyiṟ piriyēl.
Swerve not from rectitude.
4261. nilaiyai viṭṭāl nīccu.
When out of your depth, swimming.
4262. nillāta kālaṭi neṭum tūram pōkum.
Ceaseless walking accomplishes great distances.
4263. nillātu ētum nilaiyē kalvi.
Learning alone is enduring, all else is evanescent.
4264. niḻalukkum kaḷavukkum ottatu ākkai.
The body may be compared to a shadow, and to theft.
4265. niḻal arumai veyilil teriyum.
The salubrity of shade is realized in sunshine.
4266. niḻal nallatu muciṟu pollātatu.
The shade is good, the ants are bad.
4267. niṟainta āṟṟilē peruṅkāyam karaittatupōla.
Like dissolving assafoetida in a flooded river.
4268. niṟai potiyilē kaḻutai vāy vaittāṟpōla.
As an ass put its mouth into a full sack.
4269. niṟaiyak kuṟuṇi vēṇṭām talai taṭavik kuṟuṇi koṭu.
I do not want a kuruni heaped up, give me a kuruni of grain level with
the brim.
4270. niṉaikku muṉ varuvāṉ niṉaippatum taruvāṉ.
He will come before you think of him, he will give what you in-tend
applying for.
4271. niṉaitta nēram neṭu maḻai peyyumā?
Will heavy rain fall as we may wish?
4272. niṉaittatu irukka niṉaiyātatu eytum, niṉaittatu vantālum vantu nērum.
When one thing is expected another may come, and that which is thought
of may possibly come.
4273. niṉṟa marattil neṭu maram pōṉāl niṟkum maramē neṭu maram.
When the lofty trees are felled, the remaining trees look tall.
4274. niṉṟa veḷḷattaiyum vanta veḷḷam koṇṭupōyiṟṟu.
The flood that was, has been swept away by the flood that followed.
4275. niṉṟāl neṭu maram viḻuntāl paṉai maram.
When standing, a tall tree, when fallen, a palmyrah tree.
4276. niṉṟāṟpōl viḻuntāl talai uṭaiyum.
If you fall as you stand, your head will be broken.
4277. nīcarāṉavar nilaipeṟak kallār.
The base do not persevere in study.
4278. nīṭṭu vittai ēṟātu.
Boasted learning will not avail.
4279. nīṇṭa kai neruppu aḷḷum.
The stretched hand vill lade out fire.
4280. nīṇṭa taccum kuṟukiya kollum.
Length to the carpenter, shortness to the smith.
The former can easily shorten wood by cutting,-the smith canlengthen
iron by heating.
4281. nīṇṭa pul niṟka niḻalāmā?
Will long grass afford a shade?
A ready affirmation would answer this in central Africa.
4282. nīti aṟṟa paṭṭaṇattil niṟaimaḻai peyyumā?
Will sufficient rain fall in a city where justice cannot be obtained ?
4283. nīti kēḷāmal talai veṭṭuvārkaḷā?
Will they cut off the head without judicial proceedings?
4284. nītimāṉ tīviṉai ceyyiṟ piḻaippāṉā, nīti illātavaṉ nīti purintāl marippāṉā?
Will a virtuous man escape death if he do evil, will the unjust die if
he do justice?
4285. nī nīrālē vilakiṉāy nāṉ neruppālē vilakiṉēṉ.
You have escaped an accident by water, I have escaped one by fire.
4286. nīntamāṭṭātavaṉai āṟu koṇṭupōkiṟatu.
The river carries away him who cannot swim.
4287. nī piṟarkku utavi ceytāl teyvam uṉakku utavi ceyyum.
If you help others, God will help you.
4288. nīrakam poruntiya ūrakattiru.
Live in a village where there is a good supply of water.
4282 nīril eḻuttākum ākkai.
The body is an inscription on water.
4290. nīriṟ kumiḻi carīram.
The body is a bubble on water.
4291. nīrum kollum neruppum kollum.
Water kills, and fire also kills.
4292. nīrai aṭittāl vēṟāmā?
Can water be divided by a stroke ?
4293. nīraic cintiṉaiyō cīraic cintiṉaiyō?
Did you spill water, or did you spill your character?
4294. nīr āḻam kāṇalām neñcu āḻam kāṇappaṭātu.
The depth of water may be ascertained, but not the depth of the heart.
4295. nīr uyara nel uyarum.
As the water rises, the rice plant rises.
4296. nīr uḷḷa maṭṭum mīṉ kuñcu tuḷḷum.
The young fish will sport as long as the water lasts.
4297. nīr eṉṟu coṉṉāl neruppu aviyumā?
Can fire be quenched by pronouncing the word water?
4298. nīr eṉṟu colla neruppāy muṭintatu.
When it was said to be water, it turned out to be fire.
4299. nīrkkuṭ pācipōl vērkkoḷḷātu.
As moss in water, it will not take root.
4300. nīr pōṉāl mīṉ tuḷḷumā?
When the water goes, will the fish leap about?
4301. nīrmēṟ kumiḻipōl nilai illāk kāyam.
The body is unstable as a bubble on water.
4302. nīr viḷaiyāṭēl.
Do not play in water.
4303. nīlattukkuk kaṟuppu ūṭṭavēṇṭumā?
Is black dyed in blue?
4304. nīlam kaṭṭuppaṭap pēcukiṟatu.
To speak so as to confine the blue dye to the skirts of the cloth.
4305. nīlikkuk kaṇṇīr nimaiyilē.
A termagant has her tears in the eyelids.
4306. nuṭpa puttimāṉ tiṭpa cittivāṉ.
The shrewed are successful.
4307. nuṇṇiya ñāṉam uraippārkaḷ coṉṉatil oṉṟum naṭavārkaḷ.
They give wise counsels, but they themselves walk not according to any
one of them.
4308. nuṇṇiya karumamum eṇṇit tuṇi.
Although a small thing, do it after due thought.
4309. nuṇporuḷ koṭuttu nuṇṇiyarāvar.
They become possessed of discrimination by imparting instruction on
abstruse subjects.
4310. nuṇmai nukarēl.
Be not particular in food.
4311. nurai ottatuvē taraiyiṟ pavuṣu.
The affluence of this world is as froth.
4312. nuraiyait tiṉṟāl paci pōkātu.
Hunger cannot be satisfied by eating froth.
4313. nuḻai viṭṭu cey nūl kaṟṟu aṭaṅku.
Build a house with an entrance, acquire learning and be humble.
4314. nuḷaiyaṉ aṟivāṉā rattiṉattiṉ perumai?
Does a fisherman understand the value of gems?
4315. nuḷaiyaṉ pēccu ampalam ēṟātu.
The words of a fisherman will not reach the assembly.
4316. nuṉikkompil ēṟi aṭikkompu veṭṭuvārkaḷā?
Having ascended to the extremity of the bough, will they sever it from
the tree?
4317. nūl illāmal mālai kōttatupōla.
Like making a garland of flowers without a string.
4318. nūl kaṟṟavaṉē mēlavaṉ āvāṉ.
The learned only will gain eminence.
4319. nūṟu āṇṭu ākilum kalviyai nōkku.
Though a hundred years old, diligently acquire knowledge.
4320. nūṟu kuṟṟam āṟu piḻai koṇṭu poṟukkavēṇṭum.
A hundred offences must be forgiven as if only six faults.
4321. nāṟunāḷ ōti āṟunāḷ viṭat tīrum.
The learning acquired in a hundred days, will be lost by six days
neglect.
4322. nāṟōṭu nāṟu ākiṟatu neyyilē cuṭṭi paṇikāram.
Though the debt may increase to another hundred, bake the cake in ghee.
4323. nūṟṟukku iruntālum kūṟṟukku arikīrai.
Though a hundred years old, he is only a young plant to the regent(?) of
the dead.
4324. nūṟṟukku iruppār aimpatil cākār.
Those who will live to be a hundred, will not die at fifty.
4325. nūṟṟukku mēl ūṟṟu, āyirattukkumēl āṟṟupperukku.
After a hundred a spring, after a thousand a flooded river.
4326. nūṟṟukku oru pēccu āyirattukku oru talai acaippu.
When he is worth a hundred, a word, when a thousand, a mere nod.
4327. nūṟṟaik keṭuttatu kuṟuṇi.
One measure of chaff spoiled a hundred of grain.
4328. nūṉmuṟai terintu cīlattoḻuku.
Ascertaining the rules of the Veda, live virtuously.
4329. neñcu aṟiyap poy collalāmō?
May one tell a lie knowingly?
4330. neñcai oḷittu oru vañcakam illai.
There is no deceit which can be concealed from the mind.
4331. neṭiyār kuṟiyārai āṟṟilē teriyum.
It will be known in the river, who is tall and who is short.
4332. neṭum kaṭal ōṭiyum nilaiyē kalvi.
Though you may cross the broad sea your learning will remain with you.
4333. neṭum pakalukkum astamaṉam uṇṭu.
Even a long day has a sunset.
4334. neykiṟavaṉukku ēṉ kuraṅkuk kuṭṭi?
What has a weaver to do with a young monkey?
4335. neykkuṭattil eṟumpu moyttatu pōla.
As the ants swarm on a ghee pot.
4336. neykkuṭam uṭaintāl nāykku viruntu.
If the ghee pot is broken, the dog has a feast.
4337. neykkuṭam uṭaintatu nāykku vēṭṭai.
The breaking of the ghee pot, is a hunting excursion to the dog.
4338. neyyai urukkit tayiraip perukkic cāppiṭa vēṇṭum.
Use ghee after melting, and curds diluted.
4339. neruñci muḷ taittālum kuṉintu allavō piṭuṅka vēṇṭum?
Though it be but a thistle that has entered your foot, you must sit
down, must you not, to pull it out?
4340.neruppil ī moykkumā ?
Will flies swarm in fire ?
4341. neruppiṉum pollātu karippiṉ vātai.
The distress of famine is worse than that of fire.
4342. neruppilum pollāc ceruppu.
Shoes worse than fire.
4343. neruppilē puḻup paṟṟumā?
Will worms breed in fire?
4344. neruppu āṟum mayirp pālamum.
A river of fire, and a bridge of hair.
4345, neruppu illāmaṟ pukai pukaiyumā ?
Will there be smoke where there is no fire ?
4346. neruppu niṉṟa kāṭṭilē ētāvatu niṉṟālum niṟkum, nīr niṉṟa kāṭṭil oṉṟum
niṟkātu.
Something may possibly remain in a forest after a fire, but nothing
remains after a flood.
4347. neruppu eṉṟāl vāy vēkumā?
By pronouncing the word fire, will the mouth be burnt ?
4348. neruppaik kaṇṭu mitittālum cuṭum, kāṇāmal mitittālum cuṭum.
Whether you tread on fire wittingly or unawares, it will burn you.
4349. neruppaic cērnta yāvum ataṉ niṟamākum ; atupōla, periyōraic cērntavarum
āvār.
Things put into fire partake of its colour, in like manner will it
happen to those who join the great.
4350. neruppuc ciṟitu eṉṟu muṉṟāṉaiyil muṭiyalāmā?
May you tie fire in the skirt of your cloth because a mere spark ?
4351. neruppait talai kīḻāyp piṭittālum atiṉ cuvālai kīḻākumā?
Although you carry fire head downwards, will the flame burn in that
direction?
4352. nel eṭukkavum pul eṭukkavum āccō ?
Am I destined to carry both paddy and grass ?
4353. nellum uppum picaintu uṇṇak kūṭumā?
Will a mixture of paddy and salt be agreeable to the palate?
4354. nellu vakai eṇṇiṉālum paḷḷa vakai eṇṇak kūṭātu.
Though one may enumerate the various kinds of rice, he cannot enumerate
the varieties of the palla caste.
4355. nel viḷainta pūmiyum aṟiyāy, nilā eṟitta iṭamum aṟiyāy.
You do not know a rice crop, nor a moonlight space.
4356. neṟi tappuvārkku aṟivippatu vīṇ.
It is useless to teach those who diviate from rectitude.
4357. neṟpayir viḷai.
Cultivate rice.
4358. nēttira maṇiyē cūttira aṇiyē.
The apple of the eye, is an ingenious piece of mechanism.
4339. nēyamē niṟkum.
Love alone will abide.
4360. nērā nōṉpu cīr ākātu.
Fasting without a vow is not good.
4361. nērntu nērntu coṉṉālum, nīcak kacaṭar vacam ākār.
Though repeatedly told, the base take no heed.
4362. nērpaṭa oḻuku.
Behave evenly or agreeably.
4363. nēṟṟu vanta moṭṭaicci ney vārttu uṇṇac ciṇuṅkukiṟāḷ.
The bald headed woman who came but yesterday, is whining for ghee to eat
with her rice.
4364. nēṟṟu vantāṉām kuṭi avaṉ talaiyil viḻuntatām iṭi.
It is said that he took up his abode only yesterday, and he has beca
struck by lightning.
4365. naiṣatam pulavarkku oḷaṣatam.
Naishadha is a cordial to poets.
4366. naipavar eṉiṉum noyya uraiyēl.
Speak not roughly even to a beggar.
4367. naiyak kaṟṟiṉum noyya naṉku urai.
Though mollified by learning, speak effectively.
4368. naiviṉai naṇukēl.
Go not near that which is pernicious.
4369. noṭippōtum vīṇiṭēl.
Lose not even a moment of time.
4370. noṇṭi noṇṭi naṭappāṉēṉ kaṇṭataṟku ellām paṭaippāṉēṉ?
Why walk limping, and why offer oblations, to every god you see ?
4371. noṇṭik kutiraikkuc caṟukkiṉatu cākku.
To slip is the excuse of the lame horse.
4372. nonta kaṇ irukka nōkāk kaṇṇukku maruntu.
Applying medicine to the sound eye, instead of to the one diseased.
4373. nontu aṟiyātavar centamiḻ kaṟṟōr.
Those who are well versed in classical Tamil know not want.
4374. noy yarici koti poṟukkumā?
Will bruised rice bear boiling ?
4375. noṟuṅkat tiṉṟārkku nūṟu vayatu.
Those who masticate their food, live a hundred years.
4376. nōkātu uṇarvōr kalviyai nōṟpār.
Those who study unmindful of the pains attending it are devoted to
learning.
4377. nōkāmal aṭikkiṟēṉ ōyāmal aḻu.
I will beat you without giving pain, set up an unceasing cry.
4378. nōkkat totuṅku.
Be careful to secure your object.
4379. nōkka nōkkuva nōkkāmuṉ nōkkuvaṉ.
He whom we seek to see, has already seen us.
4380. nōyaṟṟa vāḻvē vāḻvu kuṟaivaṟṟa celvamē celvam.
Freedom from sickness is true happiness, and competence is true riches.
4381. nōyāḷikku ācai vārttai coṉṉāṟpōla.
Like uttering soothing words to a sick person.
4382. nōyāḷi vitiyāḷi āṉāl parikāri pērāḷi āvāṉ.
If destiny favours the patient, his doctor will obtain fame.
4383. nōyāḷikkut teriyum nōyiṉ varuttam.
The sick person knows the intensity of his suffering.
4384. nōy koṇṭār pēykoṇṭār.
The sick are like those possessed of demons.
4385. nōyñcaṟ pūṉai mattai nakkumāpōlē.
As a lean cat licks the churnstaff.
4386. nōytta puli ākilum māṭṭukku valitu.
Though the chetah is sick, it is stronger than on ox.
4387. nōlāmaiyiṉāl mēlāṉatupōm.
By neglecting religious austerities supreme good will be lost.
4388. nōvu oru pakkam irukkac cūṭu oru pakkam pōṭṭār pōla.
Like branding the side that is not affected by disease.
4389. nōṉpu eṉpatu koṉṟu tiṉṉāmai.
Not to take life for the purpose of eating, is to fast.
4390. neḷavit toḻil nalam.
Youthful education will prove beneficial.
4391. nauviyiṟṟāṉē teyvaṟivaik kal.
Know God when you are young.
4392. nauviyum mutumaiyum naṭuvu maṟṟavaṉ.
He who is without youth, old age, or manhood.
4393. nauviyum vāḻkkaiyum aḻakalla, naṟkuṇam oṉṟē aḻaku.
Youth and affluence do not form one's beauty, goodness alone does.
4394. pakaliṟ pakkam pārttup pēcu , arttarāttiriyil atu tāṉum
pēcātē.
By day, look round and speak, at midnight do not utter a word.
4395. pakaliṟ pacu teriyavillai iravil erumai teriyumā ?
Not being able to distinguish a cow by day, how can he distinguish a
buffalo by night?
